輝達領導權更迭，短期營運不至於因人事更迭立即出現結構性改變
• 輝達領導權更迭，考驗AI生態系統的延續性：輝達共同創辦人黃仁勳於11月12日正式辭任執行長及董事職務，由財務長柯
蕾特・克雷斯暫代執行長，公司董事會則啟動接班人遴選。此次人事變動不僅涉及企業治理，更可能影響全球AI產業的競爭
格局。輝達的領先優勢並非僅來自GPU晶片性能，更建立在黃仁勳主導的整合策略與扁平化管理（約60名高階主管直接向黃
仁勳匯報），包括CUDA軟體平台、高速網路、AI伺服器及完整運算系統，形成難以複製的產業生態系。
• 短期營運不至於因人事更迭立即出現結構性改變：新任領導團隊無法維持跨部門整合效率，可能影響產品迭代速度與供應鏈
協調，為AMD等競爭對手創造追趕空間。不過，輝達既有的研發團隊、CUDA開發者基礎及產品路線圖仍具延續性，短期營

運不至於因人事更迭立即出現結構性改變，後續關鍵在於接班團隊能否延續原有技術與生態系統優勢。

輝達領導層變局影響：短期業績有撐，長期考驗技術決策速度
• 投資人最關心的公司盈利能力，輝達在未來一至兩年內仍具備極高的能見度，輝達的霸主地位並非僅建立在個人魅力之上，
而是龐大工程師團隊、難以撼動的CUDA軟體生態系統，以及緊密的全球半導體供應鏈。
• 短期財務預測不變：由於既有的客戶訂單、供應鏈產能分配與產品路線圖均已確立，預期這起人事變動不會改變輝達在
2027至2028年的核心獲利預測。
• 長期繼任風險浮現：黃仁勳過去能在晶片架構、軟體、網路與系統之間做出極快速的權衡決策，並深度維繫核心客戶與供應
商關係，失去這位靈魂人物後，繼任成本不會立刻反映在財報上，但可能會在未來數個產品週期中逐步顯現，考驗新管理層
在面對超微(AMD)等競爭對手時的應變與交易推進速度。
• 華為受限於製造瓶頸，短期難撼動輝達主導地位：在硬體算力供給方面，中國本土替代方案仍面臨嚴峻技術天花板。自

2020年美國切斷華為與台積電的代工合作後，華為被迫轉向中芯國際，不僅研發週期拉長，效能提升也相對有限。預估
2026年輝達的H100等效晶片產量高達2,300萬顆，而華為昇騰(Ascend)系列產量僅約90萬顆，不足輝達的 4%。
輝達主導全球AI晶片算力 華為2026年AI晶片產量預計不足輝達的4%
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.13

美國對中國管制恐全面收緊
• 地緣政治與政策轉向：相較於公司內部營運交接，黃仁勳離任對美國出口管制政策的衝擊來得更為直接且劇烈。過去黃仁勳
是華府內部反對收緊對華晶片管制最具影響力的游說者，甚至能直接與總統川普溝通並促成政策放寬(如先前撤銷H20限制
與放行H200銷往中國)。隨著輝達在華盛頓的政治影響力減弱，美國政府與國會正加速封堵中國獲取先進AI算力的四大途徑
(本土生產、合法進口、轉運走私、海外遠程訪問)。即將於深圳舉行的APEC峰會前夕，美國強硬派立法動能增強，恐使明
年1月到期的中美貿易休戰協議更難延續，中國取得美國先進算力的管道可能在輝達內部繼任風險發酵前就已全面收緊。
• 小結與投資建議：黃仁勳的離任對全球AI產業而言是一場雙面刃衝擊，短期內，輝達憑藉深厚的技術護城河與訂單積壓，基

本面依然穩健；但中長期來看，公司將面臨領導層過渡的陣痛，同時因失去強力的華府游說管道，中國市場的營收貢獻與算
力供應鏈將面臨更嚴格的合規審查與政策封鎖，投資建議增持非中國區雲端服務商(CSP)與美國AI基礎設施標的，降低高度
依賴中國AI算力轉運或灰市貿易的周邊供應鏈。
中國獲取先進AI算力的四條途徑美國在前沿模型領域保持微弱領先
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.13

中國出口續強，高科技需求推升貿易順差突破兆美元
• 中國出口續強，AI與高科技需求推升貿易順差突破兆美元：10月進出口數據優於預期，出口年增率35.2%較9月略為放緩，但
仍維持雙位數成長，進口年增41.6%，受惠於全球AI熱潮與科技投資擴張，積體電路出口金額年增率高達137.1%，不僅遠高
於整體出口增幅，也反映中國在半導體與電子供應鏈中的重要地位持續提升，此外近期美中雙方同意將部分關稅豁免措施再
延長兩個月，市場普遍解讀為雙邊貿易關係短期將保持和諧，此舉有助於降低企業接單與出貨的不確定性，維持電子、機械
設備、汽車零組件等與美國市場連結較深的產業的短期外銷動能。
• 展望後市：在AI需求帶動、高科技產品出口強勁，以及美中關稅延長帶來的政策利多支撐下，中國外貿仍展現韌性，不過，

若全球經濟成長放緩或地緣政治風險再度升溫，出口成長動能仍可能面臨挑戰，後續需持續觀察海外需求與貿易政策變化。
10月中國進出口數據優於預期

中國PPI加速上漲掩蓋了能源和科技領域外的通縮壓力
• 中國PPI加速上漲掩蓋了能源和科技領域外的通縮壓力：中國10月通膨數據顯示，經濟價格結構出現明顯分化，PPI年增率由
9月的5.0%升至5.7%，優於市場預期，且已連續第三個月走升，反映能源價格上漲及AI硬體成本增加推動上游價格回升，但
CPI年增率卻由0.9%降至0.7%，低於市場預估，從細項來看，10月價格回升主要集中於能源與科技硬體，然而食品價格跌幅
擴大，抵銷能源帶來的漲價效果，核心CPI由1.0%降至0.8%，反映一般消費需求與服務消費動能仍不足。
• 投資建議：市場預期中國GDP平減指數有機會自2022年以來首度轉正，但若缺乏更積極的財政刺激或消費支持政策，經濟多
數領域仍將面臨通縮壓力，後續需持續關注國際原物料價格走勢，及中國內需復甦力道對通膨的影響，後市來看AI供應鏈及

科技硬體族群仍具相對受惠機會。
資料來源:bloomberg,2026.11.10
通膨支撐主要來自能源和科技產業 2026年的中國為供給驅動型再通膨
資料來源:bloomberg,2026.11.10

國安基金激勵台股上週大漲，市場暫時看淡輝達換帥消息
• 國安基金第10次進場：11/6財政部宣布進場，為台股投資人注入信心，激勵台股上週週漲逾10%。國安基金表示，美國期
中選舉後主要股市重挫，加上外資延續10月以來賣超趨勢，短期內市場信心明顯不足，啟動成立以來第10次國安基金進場。
• 護盤持股聚焦少數核心權值股：國安基金早期操作傾向分散買入20檔左右，近幾次護盤則集中台積電為首的權值股。前次
護盤(第九次)台積電資金占比高達六成，其他權值股包含鴻海、日月光、聯發科、台達電、廣達、富邦金及台塑。
• 觀點：(1)從歷史績效統計來看，國安基金進場後短期大多能激勵台股上漲，但中期績效表現分歧且波動大，顯示總體經濟
趨勢才是關鍵。(2)AI運算晶片教父黃仁勳因婚姻醜聞宣布閃辭，市場初步認為需求基本面未變，台股不跌反漲。(3)整體來

說台股企業因此黃仁勳事件的衝擊不大，短線國安基金支撐股市信心，後市仍需聚焦在總體經濟環境及企業財報。在AI投資
反覆遭到市場質疑的環境下，建議持股聚焦具全球供應鏈龍頭地位的企業，適度減碼單純由消息面支撐的個股。
資料來源：財政部、Bloomberg，兆豐銀行整理。2026.11.13。

美債發行指引微調，期限結構調整彈性提升
• 刪除關鍵措辭，釋放長債供給調整訊號：美國財政部最新季度再融資聲明表示，未來數季預計維持名目公債與浮動利率
債券（FRN）標售規模不變，但將過去慣用的「至少維持目前規模」修改為「預計維持目前規模」，反映決策彈性提高。
市場解讀此舉代表財政部已開始為未來調整長天期公債供給預留政策空間，降低長債供給僵固性的訊號明顯。
• 兼顧融資成本與需求結構成為政策核心：財政部強調將持續評估不同期限債券的供需變化、聯準會縮表進程及發債成本，
並以「長期最低融資成本」為主要原則。在財政赤字仍大、短券需求強勁背景下，未來若長天期需求改善不足，不排除
透過調整期限結構降低利息負擔與市場吸收壓力。
• 後市觀察：若2027年後長債供給增速放緩，將有助緩解期限溢酬上升壓力，但強勁就業及通膨風險仍可能推升殖利率，

對20年以上美債及投資等級債券評價形成支撐。投資上可關注2至7年期美國公債及投資級債，兼顧票息與利率風險；
長債則留意美國20年以上公債、長存續期間投資級債，以等待供給政策與利率方向更明朗。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.11.10。Note: Expectations based on survey of primary
dealers designated by the Federal Reserve Bank of New York..

民主黨期中選舉橫掃國會，但削減赤字的空間有限
• 民主黨期中選舉橫掃國會，但削減赤字的空間有限：從歷史經驗與2026年當前的財政結構來看，執政黨失去兩院主導權後，
美國財政赤字率（赤字占GDP比重）呈現短期擴張受限，但中長期難以下降的格局。截至2026財年（9月30日止），美國聯
邦赤字已達近 2兆美元（占GDP約 6.1%–6.2%），公眾持有債務突破 32兆美元（總債務破 40兆美元）。由於人口老化帶來
的社會安全（Social Security）與醫療保險（Medicare）屬於強制性支出，加上每年超過 1兆美元的債務利息負擔，兩黨在
不觸碰核心福利改革的情況下，削減赤字的空間有限。
• 美債供給壓力依然沉重：2027年新國會就任後，兩黨將圍繞企業稅、富人稅以及部分即將到期的個人稅收減免展開拉鋸。

即便民主黨推動對企業與高收入者加稅，也極可能遭到白宮行使否決權，導致開源受阻而節流難以達成。短期內，市場因預
期大規模單邊財政刺激受阻，美債殖利率可能出現階段性回落；但中長期看，6%以上的赤字率意味著美債供給壓力依然沉
重，長端美債（10年期、30年期）仍面臨較高的期限溢價。
資料來源：Silber bulletin，2026.11

顧 • AI資本支出短期壓抑現金流，亞洲供應鏈獲利動能持續擴散：美國超大型資料中心營運商未來幾年仍將持續擴大資本支出，
短期恐壓抑自由現金流，並引發市場對大型科技企業財務狀況的疑慮，導致美股科技股及亞洲科技、半導體類股短線波動。
然而，隨著全球AI資料中心建置持續推進，2027至2028年AI供應鏈需求預料仍將維持強勁，訂單與獲利動能亦持續向亞洲

美 
國 
美國消費動能維持韌性、房市復甦仍受壓抑，通膨風險與 AI 金融隱憂牽動聯
準會政策走向 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國零售銷售月增 0.2%、年增 6.8%，近三個月累計年增 6.4%；核
心零售月增 0.5%，消費支出仍具韌性。惟整體增幅放緩，後續須關注通
膨、利率及就業情勢變化。 
⚫ 11 月美國消費者信心指數由 48.0 升至 51.5，月增 7.3%，反映選後景氣
預期改善及汽油價格回落；短期與長期通膨預期分別降至 3.7%及 3.3%，
惟生活成本壓力仍存，整體信心仍偏弱。 
⚫ 10 月美國建築許可年化總數降至 129.5 萬戶，月減 7.4%；惟新屋開工
增至 130.9 萬戶，月增 9.9%，多戶住宅開工大幅回升。完工戶數月增
6.5%，顯示住宅供給持續釋出，惟高房貸利率仍抑制建商投資意願。 
⚫ 10 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，美國經濟與勞動市場維持韌性，AI 投資持
續帶動經濟活動；惟 9 月 PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨率升至 3.6%及 3.1%，
通膨上行風險仍存。多數官員支持維持利率於 3.75%至 4.00%，但認為
未來仍可能進一步升息，並關切 AI 相關融資及金融穩定風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 美國 11 月中旬擴大 AI 晶片出口管制，將多國列為高風險轉運地並限制
中國取得海外算力，顯示管制重心由晶片延伸至運算能力，恐提高中國
AI 發展成本並加劇美中科技競爭。 
⚫ 輝達代理執行長 Colette Kress 表示，未來將不再向中國銷售 Hopper 系
列 AI 晶片，包括 H200 產品，且後續財測已排除相關營收；在美國持
續收緊 AI 晶片出口管制下，市場憂心恐衝擊資料中心業務表現，惟公
司認為先進 AI 晶片需求強勁，整體成長動能仍穩健。 
⚫ 美國總統川普、中國國家主席習近平及俄羅斯總統普丁於深圳舉行歷史
性三邊峰會，就全球安全、貿易、能源及科技治理等議題交換意見。外
界認為，此次會晤反映大國溝通協調需求升高，對未來國際政治經濟秩
序及地緣局勢發展具有重要指標意義。

一、 美國—消費動能維持韌性、房市復甦仍受壓抑，通膨風險與 AI 金融隱憂牽
動聯準會政策走向 
2026 年 10 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節調整後為 7,482 億美元，較
上月增加 0.2%，年增 6.8%；近三月(8-10 月)累計銷售額年增 6.4%，消費支出仍
具支撐。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增 0.6%；進一步扣除汽車、零件及加
油站後，核心零售銷售月增 0.5%，與前月持平。主要業別中，雜項零售商、非實
體零售商、加油站及健康與個人護理用品商店銷售成長較佳，餐飲服務亦維持正
成長；惟汽車及零件經銷商、家具及家居用品店與服飾配件店銷售下滑。整體而
言，10 月零售銷售增幅雖較上月放緩，但核心零售及服務消費表現穩健，顯示美
國內需仍具支撐力道，後續仍須關注通膨、利率及就業情勢變化對消費動能之影
響。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 11 月美國消費者信心指數終值由上月的 48.0 回升
至 51.5，月增 7.3%、年增 1.0%，惟仍較今年 2 月美伊衝突爆發前低約 9%，顯示
消費者信心雖有所改善，但整體仍處低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 46.7
升至 49.4，月增 5.8%、年減 3.3%；消費者預期指數則由 48.8 升至 52.9，月增
8.4%、年增 3.7%，反映民眾對未來經濟前景的看法有所改善。調查指出，期中選
舉後，消費者預期未來分裂政府格局可能帶來政策調整，推升短期及長期企業景
氣預期，其中獨立選民改善最為明顯，民主黨支持者亦有所回升，惟共和黨支持
者的預期則轉弱。此外，汽油價格下降使低資產及部分中等資產家庭的財務狀況
改善，但持股較多的家庭對個人財務狀況的評價則有所下降，與 AI 相關因素引
發的股市波動相呼應。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨預期由 4.7%大幅降至 3.7%，
惟仍高於今年 2 月的 3.4%；長期通膨預期亦由 3.5%降至 3.3%，仍高於 2024 年
2.8%至 3.2%的區間，顯示通膨疑慮雖有所緩解，但尚未完全消除。 
2026 年 10 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數為 129.5 萬戶，較上月減少 7.4%、
較去年同期減少 8.7%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 77.2 萬戶，月減 8.7%；5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可則降至 47.5 萬戶，月減 5.2%，年減 0.4%，住宅開發規劃
仍趨審慎。另一方面，新屋開工年化數增至 130.9 萬戶，月增 9.9%、年增 2.8%，

其中單戶住宅完工增至 90.0 萬戶，月增 6.3%，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 10.4%。
整體而言，美國 10 月住宅市場需求仍受高房貸利率及住房負擔能力壓力影響，
建築許可續呈下滑，惟新屋開工及完工表現改善，顯示住宅供給仍持續釋出，後
續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住宅需求及營建成本變化對房市發展之影響。 
2026 年 10 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)第三季 GDP 成長動能增強，高
科技產品進口及 AI 相關投資維持熱絡；(2)勞動市場整體穩定並接近充分就業狀
態，失業率維持於 4.2%，薪資成長較前一年放緩；(3)9 月 PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨
率分別升至 3.6%及 3.1%，能源價格上漲、先前關稅措施及 AI 投資帶動之需求與
供給壓力，持續使通膨高於 2%目標，多數官員認為短期內上行風險仍偏高；(4)
多數官員支持將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.75%至 4.00%，以評估前次升息
及金融條件變化對經濟之影響，惟幾乎所有官員均認為未來仍可能進一步升息，
以確保通膨回落至目標水準；(5)官員普遍關切 AI 相關債務快速擴張、複雜融資
及循環融資安排可能引發之估值修正風險，並持續關注避險基金槓桿、銀行對非
銀行金融機構之間接曝險，以及網路安全等潛在金融穩定議題。 
美國川普政府於 11 月 16 日宣布進一步收緊中國取得輝達（Nvidia）等企業
先進人工智慧（AI）晶片的管道，將新加坡、泰國、馬來西亞、印尼及巴西列為
高風險轉運國並要求出口許可，同時限制向中國企業提供大量算力的海外資料中
心取得受管制晶片。此舉正值美國總統川普前往亞洲、並於 11 月 19 日在深圳與
中國國家主席習近平會晤前後，凸顯華府在防堵晶片轉運與遠端算力租用漏洞上
的政策動向。相較過去主要限制實體晶片出口，新措施進一步強化對海外資料中
心及 AI 算力使用的監管，反映美國持續將管制重點由硬體設備延伸至運算能力
本身，以限制中國取得高階 AI 模型訓練與推理所需資源。市場預期短期內恐提
高中國科技企業取得先進算力的成本與難度，並加速中國發展國產 AI 晶片及自
主算力基礎設施；另一方面，美中科技競爭進一步升溫，後續相關出口管制範圍、
執行細節及北京的反制措施，將持續牽動全球半導體、雲端服務及 AI 產業供應
鏈布局。

美國政府核准出口的 Hopper 系列資料中心產品，包括 H200 人工智慧晶片，此舉
被視為輝達對中國市場策略的重要轉變。輝達第三季仍向中國銷售約 24 億美元
Hopper 產品，但未來財測已不再納入相關出貨收入；同時，公司宣布成立董事會
層級委員會，負責監督國家安全政策、出口管制合規及全球貿易風險。在美國近
期進一步強化對中國 AI 晶片及跨境算力服務管制之際，市場關注新措施對輝達
營運的影響。投資銀行 Jefferies 估計，新規可能使輝達明年資料中心業務營收減
少 440 億至 620 億美元；不過公司認為，受惠於 Vera Rubin 等先進 AI 晶片需求
強勁，整體營收成長動能仍可望維持穩健。 
美國總統川普、中國國家主席習近平及俄羅斯總統普丁於深圳舉行歷史性三
邊高峰會，成為近年國際政治最受矚目的外交活動之一。此次會談聚焦全球安全
情勢、經貿合作、能源供應、AI 治理、科技競爭及區域衝突等重大議題，並就俄
烏戰爭、中東局勢、美中貿易關係、半導體出口管制及全球供應鏈穩定等議題交
換意見。市場尤其關注美中雙方是否於關稅措施、科技管制及投資限制等爭議領
域釋出緩和訊號，以及美俄是否就地緣安全與能源市場穩定達成進一步共識。分
析人士指出，本次峰會反映主要大國透過高層直接對話管控分歧、降低衝突風險
的意願升高，亦凸顯全球治理架構正朝多極化方向發展。儘管三方在烏克蘭戰爭、
台海安全、軍事部署及高科技競爭等核心議題上仍存在明顯立場差異，短期內達
成實質突破的難度仍高，惟此次會晤有助維持主要大國溝通管道，並為後續經貿、
能源及安全事務協調奠定基礎。峰會成果及相關聯合聲明內容，將持續受到國際
社會、金融市場及企業界高度關注，其後續發展亦可能對全球政治經濟秩序產生
深遠影響。 
二、歐元區—工業生產、營造表現皆暫時回溫 
 歐元區 9 月工業生產月增 0.2%，中止連續三個月的衰退，主要是車廠開始回
補汽車庫存，帶動汽車生產反彈，使耐久消費財產出月增 1.3%，抵銷因零售需求
降溫，導致能源(包含燃油等)、非耐久消費財產出月減 1.7%、0.4%的影響。另外，
9 月營造業產出回升 0.1%，中止連續四個月的衰退局面，不過，營造業持續面臨
能源與建材成本上漲，加上法國與西班牙等核心經濟體的房建與公共預算受限，
使營造業的疲軟局面暫時難以扭轉。 
 儘管歐元區 9 月工業與營造業產出止跌回升，但復甦基礎仍受到國際政經不

三、日本—出口持續改善，通膨壓力強化升息預期 
 9 月核心機械訂單(扣除波動訂單)月減 0.3%，第三季核心機械訂單季增 1.2%，
連續第四季成長，顯示企業資本支出動能仍然穩健。其中製造業是主要成長引擎，
AI 與半導體需求正逐步朝更廣泛的製造業擴散，化學產品及商用機械訂單分別成
長 57.0%及 80.5%，反映半導體材料及設備需求依然旺盛。相較之下，非製造業
資本支出動能較為溫和，反映成本上升導致企業調整部分投資計畫，惟勞動市場
結構性問題仍將支撐自動化與生產力相關投資。整體而言，日本資本支出週期尚
未出現明顯轉弱跡象，在 AI 與半導體投資外溢效應帶動下，預期資本支出仍可
維持穩健成長。 
 10 月出口年增 20.5%，其中汽車出口成長 20.0%，成為主要推動力，反映先
前災害造成的供應中斷逐步消退；半導體相關出口成長 49.7%，對中國以外的亞
洲市場科技產品出口更創歷史新高，顯示 AI 與資本支出相關需求持續強勁。從
地區來看，對歐盟、美國及中國出口均成長，其中對美出口已回升至 2025 年 2 月
以來高點。整體而言，出口動能持續改善，汽車出口復甦、AI、資本支出相關需
求可望成為後續出口的重要支撐。 
 10 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.3%，顯示通膨動能持續增強，主要受到
石油產品價格上漲持續轉嫁至商品價格，且漲價壓力已由商品逐步擴散至食品及
服務。雖然政府釋出大米儲備、高中免學費及能源補貼等措施壓低部分價格，使
米價及學費明顯下跌，但剔除生鮮食品後的核心通膨仍達 2.2%，高於央行 2%的
通膨目標。展望未來，隨汽油補貼自 12 月起逐步縮減，加上食品業者可能於 2027
年 4 月消費稅減免生效前提前調漲價格，預期 2027 年通膨壓力將進一步升高，
進而促使日本央行持續升息。

展望未來，房地產的調整週期可能會延續至 2028 年後，加上去產能政策與供
應鏈外移壓抑民間投資，消費與投資動能料將維持疲軟。在出口方面，近期 APEC
峰會促成中加、中澳經貿破冰並推進亞太自貿區，有助支撐出口表現。然而，美
中貿易關係在川習會後依然沒有重大突破，明年一月美中關稅休戰期滿，仍為貿
易環境帶來不確定因素。

(1)新加坡：10 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 79.8%，連續第 24 個月成長並
創史上最大增幅，其中，人工智慧相關需求的強勁成長，持續帶動電子產品出口
成長 73.9%，以 磁碟媒體產品（189.6%）、個人電腦（81.0%）和積體電路（53.1%）
成長最顯著；另在船舶(因向巴西石油公司交付海上浮式生產儲卸油船)、藥品
（253.1%）和專用機械（57.3%）帶動下，非電子產品出口大增 81.9%。對美國和
中國的出口分別激增 214.6%和 144.9%，其次是歐盟（90.8%）、台灣（79.3%）
和香港（65.4%），但對印度（-35.8%）和印尼（-19.0%）的出口有所下降。整體
而言，新加坡出口雖繳出亮麗成績單，惟隨美國選後 AI 基礎建設可能放緩，加
上近期因輝達黃仁勳桃色風波，美國商務部將新加坡列入晶片出口管制國家，恐
影響後續出口表現。

(2)印尼：印尼央行利率會議意外升息 1 碼(BI rate)至 6%，貸款利率和隔夜存款利
率分別調高 1 碼至 6.75%和 5.25%，旨在全球經濟情勢也充滿不確定性、印尼貨
幣面臨新一輪壓力下，採取先發制人動作，力求匯率穩定並使通膨率控制在
2.5%±1%的目標區間內，央行同時維持對 2026 年 GDP 成長 4.9%至 5.7%的預測。
展望未來，維持外匯穩定仍是印尼央行的首要政策重點，在通膨居高不下、美國
聯準會升息、美元走強，資金外流壓力仍高下，未來仍有進一步升息風險。 
(3)泰國：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 2.9%，主要得益於全球對人工智慧
相關產品的強勁需求，帶動出口大幅成長 18.3%，同時，進口成長放緩至 16.3%，
放大淨出口對經濟的貢獻；私人消費（2.5%）和固定投資（11.8%）成長速度加
快，惟政府支出四個季度以來首次下降（-1.6%）。按產業別來看，製造業產出成
長 3.7%為主要貢獻來源、運輸和倉儲業（3.1%）、批發和零售業（6.0%）的產出
成長加速，但旅遊業依然疲軟。泰國政府將今年的 GDP 成長預期從先前的 2.0%-
2.5%上調至 2.5%，高於 2025 年的 2.4%。 
(4)馬來西亞：10 月出口額年增 61.1%，達 MYR 2,392 億元的歷史新高，持續反
映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，電子電氣產品出口增幅達 87.2%，礦產品出口亦大幅成長
37.0%，抵銷農產品出口下降 6.1%的影響，對美國、台灣、新加坡出口分別激增
108%、293%、55%。10 月進口額年增 54.4%至 MYR 1,974 億元，其中，中間產
品（64.0%）、資本貨物（32.9%）、消費品（11.8%）等進口均有成長，按產業別
觀之，製造業、礦業進口額分別成長 60.6%、44.2%，本月自印度（119.6%）、韓
國（194.0%）進口呈倍數成長，自中國、東協進口亦成長 56.5%、64.8%。展望未
來，美國商務部 11/16 發布新規，將新加坡、馬來西亞、泰國、印尼及巴西等五
國列為晶片轉運高風險國家，要求先進 AI 晶片出口至上述國家的部分數據中心
須事先取得許可，並進一步限制中國企業透過海外數據中心遠端租用算力。由於
馬來西亞資料中心容量多屬「共置（colocation）」型態，中國企業占比逾半數，
一旦中國企業難以透過馬國及周邊國家租用高階算力，將影響中國企業投資意
願，加上合規成本增加，將影響未來出口及經濟表現。

2357 華碩電腦(ASUS)

報告日期：2026/11/17 研究員：曾智楷(Kevin Tseng) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 品牌營收 2588 億，QoQ +7% ，毛利率 15.9.6%，EPS 
29.10 元。 
 公司預估 4Q26 伺服器營收將季增 100%，由於部分客戶第三
季因建置時程延後，使伺服器出貨與營收遞延至第四季認列。
PC 與零組件業務方面，受到記憶體等零組件價格上漲壓抑終
端市場需求，預估 4Q26 電腦業務營收季減 15%，零組件則預
估將季減 10%。 
 受惠於 GPU AI 伺服器需求暢旺，公司預估 2026 全年伺服器
營收將成長 150%，並 預 估 2027 年伺服器營收可望維持雙位數
至高雙位數的年增率。

一. 受惠伺服器、PC 業務成長，3Q26 EPS 29.1 元 
華碩 3Q26 品牌營收 2587.9 億元，QoQ +7%，YoY +36%，主要
受惠於伺服器、PC 業務成長，帶動第三季營收創歷史新高。就產
品組合來看，3Q26 系統產品(System)佔整體營收的 50%，開放
平台(Open Platform) 佔 20%，基礎設施解決方案事業群(IS)佔
30%。公司表示，面對 DRAM 等零組件漲價逆風，第三季 PC 營
收仍年增超過 25% ，主要由於公司高價值產品，包含電競
Gaming、高階 AI PC 及商用 PC，佔 PC 營收比重超過 65%，且
商用 PC 出貨量維持強勁，年成長超過 30%。另外，伺服器業務
方面，儘管部分訂單遞延至第四季認列，第三季伺服器營收仍實
現超過 100%的年增長；通用型 CPU 伺服器亦受惠於企業擴建地
端資料中心以支援 AI 工作負載，營收年增超過 80%。獲利方面，
3Q26 營業毛利 412 億，QoQ +3%，YoY +68%，毛利率 15.9%，
QoQ -0.6ppts，YoY +3.0ppts，營業利益 219.6 億，QoQ +12%，
YoY +161%，營利率 8.5%，QoQ +0.4ppts，YoY +4.1ppts，3Q26
業外利益 35.9 億，稅後淨利 216.3 億，QoQ +14%，YoY +105%，
單季稅後 EPS 29.1 元。

二. 4Q26 伺服器遞延訂單放量抵銷 PC 逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 1076 億元，MoM +10.7%，YoY +62.2%。今
年累計營收 8500 億，YoY +41.1% 。展望 4Q26，受惠於伺服器
呈現強勁成長，公司預估 4Q26 伺服器營收季增 100%，年增
280%，主要由於部分資料中心客戶於第三季因建置時程延後，使
伺服器出貨與營收遞延至第四季認列。而 PC 與零組件業務方面，

受到記憶體等零組件價格上漲推升終端 ASP 影響，壓抑終端市場
需求，公司預估 4Q26 電腦業務營收季減 15%，年增 15%。零組

三. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 89.30/86.35 元 
展望 2026 年，在伺服器業務方面，公司表示目前整體訂單能見度
強勁，除了受惠於 GPU AI 伺服器需求暢旺之外，企業擴建地端資
料中心，亦帶動通用型 CPU 伺服器需求同步提升。在第四季翻倍
季增帶動下，公司預估 2026 全年伺服器營收達成至少 150%的年
成長目標。而 PC 與零組件業務，則受記憶體等零組件價格大幅
上漲影響，在 4Q26 至 1H27 的 PC 市場需求逆風與價格成本混亂
期間，PC 業務的獲利能力與營業利益率將承受一定壓力。預估公
司 2026 全年營收 1.05 兆，YoY +42.5%，毛利率 15.8%，營利率
7.2%，稅後淨利 663 億，稅後 EPS 89.30 元。展望 2027 年，PC
整體市場受零組件價格上漲與需求定價混亂影響，公司預估 2027
年全球 PC 市場整體出貨量將年減 10%至 15%，就季度節奏來看，
上半年跌幅較大，下半年將逐步回升。而伺服器業務方面，管理
層則預估 2027 年伺服器營收可望維持雙位數至高雙位數的年增
率。由於公司客戶涵蓋 Tier 2/3 CSP、NeoCloud、大型科技公司、
AI 研究機構、企業地端資料中心及國家級主權 AI 專案，儘管近期
市場面臨資本市場融資條件趨緊以及美國期中大選後各地資料中
心建置監管趨嚴等逆風，但目前訂單需求並未出現明顯延遲或變
化。預估公司 2027 全年營收 1.26 兆，YoY +19.5% ，毛利率
14.5%，營利率 6.6%，稅後淨利 641 億，稅後 EPS 86.35 元。

四. 投資建議為 750~950 元區間操作 
公司 AI 伺服器業務維持強勁成長動能，預期 2027 年將維持雙位
數成長表現，但考量到記憶體缺貨情況延續，可能持續影響公司
PC 業務的營運動能。公司目前股價交易於 2027 EPS 約 9~10 倍
本益比，評價上並未明顯偏低，給予區間操作投資建議，目標價
750-950 元(9-11x 2027 EPS)。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
華碩電腦(ASUS)為全球知名筆電品牌，以自有品牌「ASUS」銷
售全球。主要營業項目為 3C 資訊產品(含電腦系統產品、主機板
及各類板卡及智慧型手機等手持裝置等)之設計、研發及銷售。

二.產業趨勢概況 
1.3Q26 全球 PC 出貨量暴跌逾二成，AI 搶走記憶體成衰退元兇 
受 AI 資料中心擴張排擠記憶體與零組件供給影響，2026 年第三
季全球 PC 市場旺季不旺，根據 IDC，3Q26 PC 單季出貨量降至
6,270 萬台，年減 20.1%。由於 DRAM 與 SSD 價格暴漲逾 4 倍，
在 PC 成本占比由 15%飆升至近 40%，加上處理器與顯示卡等元
件短缺，以及業者於上半年提前拉貨透支需求，導致整體市場面
臨沉重的庫存與成本壓力。就各大品牌來看，第三季普遍陷入雙
位數的衰退，聯想第三季出貨量年減-22.6%、惠普-30.9%、戴爾
-25%、蘋果-11.3%，而華碩則展現出強勁的韌性，第三季出貨量
達 550 萬台，年減 8.6%，為全球前五大 PC 品牌中唯一跌幅控制
在個位數的業者，表現明顯優於其他競爭對手。然而研究機構仍

2. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

3. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 4Q26 伺服器遞延訂單放量抵銷 PC 逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 1076 億元，MoM +10.7%，YoY +62.2%。今
年累計營收 8500 億，YoY +41.1% 。展望 4Q26，受惠於伺服器
呈現強勁成長，公司預估 4Q26 伺服器營收季增 100%，年增
280%，主要由於部分資料中心客戶於第三季因建置時程延後，使
伺服器出貨與營收遞延至第四季認列。而 PC 與零組件業務方面，
受到記憶體等零組件價格上漲推升終端 ASP 影響，壓抑終端市場
需求，公司預估 4Q26 電腦業務營收季減 15%，年增 15%。零組
件則預估將季減 10%，年減 15%。預估公司 4Q26 營收 3106 億

二. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 89.30/86.35 元 
展望 2026 年，在伺服器業務方面，公司表示目前整體訂單能見度
強勁，除了受惠於 GPU AI 伺服器需求暢旺之外，企業擴建地端資
料中心，亦帶動通用型 CPU 伺服器需求同步提升。在第四季翻倍
季增帶動下，公司預估 2026 全年伺服器營收達成至少 150%的年
成長目標。而 PC 與零組件業務，則受記憶體等零組件價格大幅
上漲影響，在 4Q26 至 1H27 的 PC 市場需求逆風與價格成本混亂
期間，PC 業務的獲利能力與營業利益率將承受一定壓力。預估公
司 2026 全年營收 1.05 兆，YoY +42.5%，毛利率 15.8%，營利率
7.2%，稅後淨利 663 億，稅後 EPS 89.30 元。展望 2027 年，PC
整體市場受零組件價格上漲與需求定價混亂影響，公司預估 2027
年全球 PC 市場整體出貨量將年減 10%至 15%，就季度節奏來看，
上半年跌幅較大，下半年將逐步回升。而伺服器業務方面，管理
層則預估 2027 年伺服器營收可望維持雙位數至高雙位數的年增
率。由於公司客戶涵蓋 Tier 2/3 CSP、NeoCloud、大型科技公司、
AI 研究機構、企業地端資料中心及國家級主權 AI 專案，儘管近期
市場面臨資本市場融資條件趨緊以及美國期中大選後各地資料中
心建置監管趨嚴等逆風，但目前訂單需求並未出現明顯延遲或變
化。預估公司 2027 全年營收 1.26 兆，YoY +19.5% ，毛利率
14.5%，營利率 6.6%，稅後淨利 641 億，稅後 EPS 86.35 元。

### Page 1

M
Update
King Yuan Electronics Co Ltd  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Continuing capacity build with unchanged 2027 demand: The company mentioned
that AI demand remains strong and customers (especially the largest customer) are
still asking for more clean room build. 2027 demand is almost confirmed with no
changes over the past month.
Capacity allocation: US capacity will fully support the largest customer initially and
the capacity in Taiwan is fungible between different customers. For example, given

4Q26 weakness for consumer/smartphones, the company has shifted spare capacity
to meet other demand, including the testers from Advantest and Teradyne.
Margin should still be in the upward trend: The priority for the company now is to
have customers consign the equipment if they are asking for more capacity, lowering
the deprecation pressure. Also, given the tight capacity, the company would serve
the products with higher margin (generally AI demand and longer test time product)

the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
King Yuan Electronics Co Ltd (2449.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity of 9.8%, intermediate-term
growth rate of 10.5%, terminal growth rate of 3.0%, and cash dividend payout ratio of 65%.
Risks to Upside
n Rise in shipments for core customers such as MediaTek
n Strong AI demand from both GPU and ASIC
n Penetration of wafer sorting business
n Rise in IDM outsourcing to OSAT

Risks to Downside
n Overall non-AI demand remaining weak
n Significant testing market share loss
n Drop in shipments for core customers such as MediaTek

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

3217.TT 優群 
 消費電子偏弱，靜待伺服器新品放量

[財務資訊更新]   
   公司簡介 
優群成立於 1987 年，總部位於新竹，主要從事電子零組件及連接器的研發、設計與
製造，產品涵蓋 DDR、M.2、USB Type-C 及微型沖壓件，應用於 PC、伺服器、網通、
手機及車用電子等領域，並積極布局高頻高速傳輸的連接器技術。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
區間操作 114 
【前次投資建議】買進，234 
【大盤指數/股價】36033 / 95.4

➢ 3Q26 記憶體價格高漲致客戶短料，EPS 2.84 元，低於預期 
3Q26 營收 9.75 億元，QoQ+2.9%、YoY-13.8%，受 DRAM 缺貨漲價
促使筆電採用板載設計影響，SO-DIMM 及 M.2 銷售年減 36%及 20%。
CAMM 出貨雖季增 10 倍，仍不足以彌補缺口；毛利率 46.06%，QoQ-
1.5ppts，YoY-5.1ppts，主因產能利用率下降及產品組合轉差；EPS 2.84
元，YoY-32.8%。營收與獲利皆低於投研部預期。 
➢ 4Q26 消費電子續弱但伺服器占比漸升，衰退幅度將逐步收斂 
10 月營收 2.58 億元，MoM-22.8%、YoY-25.9%，主要受筆電及消費性
電子需求疲弱影響。展望 4Q26，Long-DIMM、Type-C、微型沖壓件可
望延續 3Q26 的成長，伺服器占比將逐步增加，此外電信通訊產品亦可
望挹注營收。投研部預估 4Q26 營收 8.25 億元，QoQ-15.4%、YoY-
20.7%，毛利率 44.75%，QoQ-1.3ppts，YoY-4.2ppts，但衰退幅度將
收斂；稅後 EPS 2.31 元，QoQ-18.4%，YoY-28.4%。 
➢ 2027 Google 伺服器新案受矚，營運有望重返成長 
展望 2027 年，優群將積極降低對筆電市場的依賴，預估筆電營收占比
由 60%降至 50%，伺服器與桌機合計提升至 20%，電信通訊增至 10%。
主要成長動能來自 Google 伺服器新專案預計 1Q27 量產、越南廠導入
Long-DIMM 及微型沖 壓 件，以及電信通訊產品 持 續放量。此外，
CAMM2 受惠 HP 新機於 1H27 陸續導入，可望帶動出貨；SOCAMM2
則已進入客戶送樣測試階段，若順利通過驗證，有望切入 AI 伺服器供應
鏈，進一步提升高階產品營收比重。 
➢ 預估 2027 年 EPS 11.47 元，建議區間偏操作，目標價 114 元 
預估 2026 年/2027 年營收 36.95 億元/40.46 億元，YoY-10.9% /+9.5%；
毛利率為 46.88% /47.31%，YoY-3ppts /+0.4ppts；稅後 EPS 10.35 元
/11.47 元，YoY-18.6% /+11%，目前 PER 約在 9.2X /8.3X，參考過往
PER 區間多位於 10X~18X，目前股價以 2027 年衡量低於區間下緣，公
司雖因 Google 新案產品將於 1Q27 量產而貢獻伺服器營收，但消費電
子成長力道仍偏弱，且近期整體盤勢波動劇烈，故投研部建議區間偏多
操作，目標價 114 元(2027F PE 10X)。

個股報告

3Q26 記憶體價格高漲衝擊插槽需求且客戶模組短缺，EPS 2.84 元，低於預期 
優群 3Q26 營收 9.75 億元，QoQ+2.9%，YoY-13.8%，雖受季節性因素而季增，但
低於投研部預期並呈現年衰退，因記憶體原廠優先供應 AI 伺服器與 HBM，導致一般
DRAM 缺貨漲價，迫使筆電品牌改採直接焊接的板載(On-board)設計，排擠記憶體
插槽需求。在此衝擊下，主力產品 SO-DIMM 與 M.2 銷售額 YoY 分別為-36%與-
20%，再加上次世代 CAMM 連接器出貨雖季增 10 倍，仍受客戶端記憶體模組短缺
限制，無法完全填補傳統產品線缺口；毛利率 46.06%，QoQ-1.5ppts，YoY-5.1ppts，
營收規模縮減直接導致工廠產能利用率下降，單位固定製造成本分攤墊高、越南新廠
為初期營運而不具規模優勢，以及傳統高毛利的 SO-DIMM 銷售比重縮水但 Type-C
與微型沖壓件等其他成長中的產品線在組合變化下，拖累整體毛利率；稅後 EPS 2.84
元，QoQ+18.9%，YoY-32.8%，亦低於預期。 
圖 1：優群產品營收佔比(依季度)

資料來源：國票投顧整理 
4Q26 消費電子續弱但伺服器占比逐步上升，衰退幅度將逐步收斂 
優群 10 月營收為 2.58 億元，MoM-22.8%、YoY-25.9%，月營收明顯低於 3Q26 單
月平均水準，主因受筆電及消費性電子需求下滑衝擊，客戶拉貨動能轉弱，使公司於
4Q26 初的出貨表現相對保守。展望後續，Long-DIMM、Type-C 連接器及微型沖壓
件等產品可望延續 3Q26 的成長趨勢，伺服器與桌機相關業務亦逐步增加；此外，電
信通訊產品已於 2026 年 9 月開始出貨，預期將持續挹注營收，成為目前新的成長動
能。投研部預估，隨著 11 月及 12 月營收年減幅度較 10 月收斂，4Q26 營收為 8.25
億元，QoQ-15.4%，YoY-20.7%；因筆電相關產品出貨減少，使工廠稼動率下降，固
定製造成本分攤效率轉差，加上產品組合改變及部分客戶要求價格支援，使毛利率持
續承壓，預估為 44.75%，QoQ-1.3ppts，YoY-4.2ppts；稅後 EPS 2.31 元，QoQ-
18.4%，YoY-28.4%。 
優群開發多項新品，領域拓展至 AI PC 與伺服器所需的高頻高速連接器 
優群目前開發中的產品眾多，包含如下： 
1) LPDDR5 CAMM2 666p SMT Solutions：CAMM2 為新一代壓接式記憶體模組架
構，主要應用於高階筆電、AI PC 及工作站，而公司開發的 666-pin SMT 連接器方
案，搭配上蓋與背板設計，可強化模組固定與接觸穩定性，並支援高密度記憶體配置，
相較傳統 SO-DIMM，CAMM2 有助於提升主機板空間利用效率及記憶體擴充彈性。

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
2) LP5 SOCAMM2：應用於 AI 伺服器與高效能運算(HPC)的新型壓接式記憶體模組，
採用 LPDDR5 記憶體，兼具低功耗、高頻寬與模組化設計等特性，而公司開發的
SOCAMM2 連接器採 694-pin、1.0mm 高度規格，可支援高密度記憶體配置，並具
備模組拆裝及維護彈性，未來有機會隨 AI 伺服器平台升級而擴大應用。 
3) M.2 Gen 6：新一代 M.2 高速連接器，主要對應未來高效能 SSD 及高速儲存裝置
的介面升級需求。隨著 AI 運算及資料中心持續擴大，系統對儲存裝置的傳輸頻寬、訊
號完整性與連接穩定性要求也將提高，促使連接器朝更高速規格發展，而公司目前已
將 M.2 Gen 6 列為開發中產品，預期可延續既有 M.2 Gen 4、Gen 5 的技術與客戶基
礎，布局下一世代高速儲存市場。 
4) DDR DIMM Socket - DDR6 426p：此插槽為因應下一世代 DDR6 記憶體規格所
開發的插槽連接器，主要應用於未來伺服器及高效能運算平台。隨著記憶體傳輸速度
與資料頻寬持續提升，連接器必須具備更高的訊號完整性、接觸可靠度及機構穩定性，
而公司具備 DDR5 RDIMM、UDIMM 插槽開發經驗，目前正在開發 426-pin DDR6 
DIMM Socket。 
5) 7A×48V (336W)大電流/高功率型 Type-C：公司針對高功耗設備開發的連接器產
品，透過提升端子導電能力與散熱性能，支援最高 7A 電流及 48V 電壓，理論供電功
率達 336W，高於 USB PD 3.1 EPR 標準的 240W，可望應用於高效能筆電、工作站、
伺服器及工業設備，滿足高功率供電與快速充電需求。 
6)可拆卸式/模組化 Type-C 連接器：採用連接器與轉接座成對使用的設計，提供板上
式與沉板式兩種組合，客戶可依產品內部空間與機構需求彈性選用，此設計除支援
USB-C 高速訊號傳輸外，也具備模組化、更換便利及配置彈性等優勢，可降低維修與
更換成本，並滿足筆電、伺服器及新世代電子設備對高速傳輸與輕薄化設計的需求。 
整體而言，這些產品以布局高階筆電、AI 伺服器、高速儲存與次世代記憶體市場為主，
反映優群正由傳統 NB 用 SO-DIMM、M.2 連接器，逐步拓展至 AI PC 與伺服器所需
的高密度、高頻高速連接器。 
2027 Google 伺服器新案受矚，營運有望重返成長 
展望 2027 年，優群的營運結構可望逐步擺脫過去高度依賴筆電市場的情況，並朝伺
服器、電信通訊及高階記憶體連接器等領域轉型。由於 2027 年筆電市場仍可能受到
記憶體缺料、價格上漲及終端需求疲弱等因素影響，品牌客戶對筆電產品的拉貨態度
預期仍相對保守，筆電相關營收恐面臨衰退壓力，因此公司將積極調整產品組合，降
低單一應用市場波動對整體營運的影響。投研部預估，筆電營收占比將由目前約 60%
下降至 2027 年的 50%，桌機與伺服器合計占比則由 14%提升至 20%，電信通訊占
比由 5%提高至 10%，OSAT 占比亦可望由約 12%提升至 12.5%，其中伺服器與電信
通訊兩項業務合計占比將達 30%，成為 2027 年營收成長的重要支撐。在伺服器業務
方面，公司既有 CSP 客戶包括 Meta 與 Google，其中 Google 新專案預計於 2026
年 11 月進行試產，並於 1Q27 透過越南廠正式量產，由於該專案規模相對較大，預
期隨著訂單逐步放量，將對伺服器相關營收帶來實質挹注。目前越南廠已承接 DDR5 
SO-DIMM 及 M.2 等標準產品，未來則將陸續導入 Long-DIMM、Google 伺服器新
專案及微型沖壓件，並規劃於 2H27 逐步將微型沖壓件產能移入越南廠，使越南廠的
產品布局由既有記憶體及儲存裝置連接器，進一步延伸至伺服器相關產品，藉此擴大
生產規模、提升產能利用率，同時配合客戶實踐 China+1 策略及供應鏈分散化的需
求，強化公司在國際伺服器供應鏈中的競爭優勢。

### Page 4

- 4 -

個股報告 
此外，電信通訊也是優群 2027 年值得關注的成長動能，公司相關產品已於 2026 年
9 月開始出貨，4Q26 持續貢獻營收，代表該業務已逐步由前期產品開發及客戶導入
階段進入實際出貨期，預期 2027 年隨著客戶需求擴大及訂單持續增加，營收貢獻將
進一步提升，故投研部預估其占比將由 2026 年的 5%倍增至 2027 年的 10%，顯示
電信通訊產品有機會成為公司未來降低筆電業務依賴、改善營收結構的重要成長來
源。 
另一方面，高階記憶體連接器方面，CAMM2 及 SOCAMM2 也是優群未來值得追蹤
的產品。CAMM2 主要應用於高階筆電、工作站及部分新型記憶體架構，公司於 3Q26
出貨量已較前期大幅成長，且 HP 多款新機種預計於 1Q27 至 2Q27 陸續導入，有助
於帶動後續出貨；至於 SOCAMM2，則是公司持續布局的高階伺服器記憶體連接器，
主要受惠於 AI 伺服器對高頻寬、高密度及高效能記憶體架構的需求提升，目前產品
已進入客戶送樣與測試階段，未來若能順利通過大型 CSP 客戶驗證並取得正式訂單，
將有機會進一步切入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，為公司帶來新的產品成長動能。 
總體而言，優群 2027 年的營運成長將主要來自伺服器與電信通訊業務擴張、Google
新專案量產，以及 CAMM2 等高階記憶體連接器持續導入，其中 Google 專案與電信
通訊產品具有相對明確的出貨時程，而 SOCAMM2 則屬於中長期成長機會；若越南
廠新產品導入順利，加上 CSP 客戶訂單持續增加，將有助於公司逐步降低筆電市場景
氣波動的影響。 
圖 2：優群產品營收佔比(依年度)

於 1Q27 量產，帶動伺服器占比升至 2 成，但考量消費電子相關營收成長力道仍偏
弱，且近期因市場對 AI 前景存有疑慮致整體盤勢波動劇烈，故投研部建議區間偏多
操作，目標價 114 元(2027F PE 10X)。

### Page 1

個股聚焦  
2026/11/23

陳奕均 fion.chen@sinopac.com

禾榮科 (7799 TT) 
獲利成長列車即將啟航

永豐觀點 
台灣藥證年底取證在即，1H27 起設備陸續商轉，營收進入兌現期，隨全球佈局深
化與自製設備落地，禾榮科技術、平台、生態綜效全面釋放，獲利加速成長期將至。 
 投資評價與建議 
建議買進：禾榮科自 2027 年起將迎來營運轉捩點，正式從臨床驗證走向實質營收
兌現。隨著適應症拓展、區域市場放量、複合式療法推進至一線治療，禾榮科的全
球商業模式將加速複製，在設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利
將迎來跳躍式增長，建議買進，目標價以 NPV 估算(WACC=6%，永續性獲利成長
10%)為 825 元。

營運現況與分析 
公司簡介：禾榮科技成立於 2017 年，為一專注加速器型硼中子捕獲治療系統（AB-
BNCT）技術開發與商業化廠商，並以成為 BNCT 領域值得信賴之全球品牌，提供
癌症精準治療，成為 BNCT 全方位解決方案提供者為目標。公司擁有完整研發、製
造與經營管理團隊，成員具多元專業背景，主要股東為泛漢民集團。在資金、營運
到研發管理團隊陣容堅強下，實力強大有利公司在高資本癌症高階醫療設備治療市
場之拓展。 
台灣藥證年底取證在即，1H27 營收正式入帳，獲利加速成長：禾榮科 BNCT 設備
已於 2024 年 11 月取證，硼中子捕獲治療用藥 BPA 藥證申請中，研究部預計今年
底前將取證，1H27 起，隨北榮設備商轉並進行病患收受，南京明碁醫院設備依完
工比例認列營收，及中國醫持續進行相關適應症之臨床試驗與正式治療等多項業務
併進下，營收將進入兌現期，預估 2027 年營收可達 29.6 億元，2028 年在南京明
碁醫院設備續依完工比例認列營收，新加坡、竹銘等國內外 BOT 案接棒商轉挹注
下，預估營收將持續跳增至 41.2 億元(+39.3YoY)，獲利成長列車正式開動。 
圖一：禾榮科 2027 年起將進入營收兌現期

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

### Page 2

2

技術領先 x 完整平台 x 生態精準，禾榮科構築 BNCT 行業壁壘：硼中子捕獲治療
（BNCT）相較於傳統重粒子治療（每人平均需 10-20 次），僅需 1 次療程即可
完成，具備顯著的臨床優勢。目前全球除日本住友（Sumitomo）設備已於日本商
轉外，禾榮科的 AB-BNCT 系統也於 2024 年順利取得 TFDA 醫療器材認證，高
居全球開發進度第二快之廠商，且相較住友及其餘臨床中競品，禾榮科 AB-BNCT
為高效率超熱中子產生系統，質子轉換效率(Proton Efficiency)達 4.8，為競爭對手
4 倍以上，質子電流與功率最低，運轉成本亦為最低，由於中子產生效率高，治療
室中殘留的輻射劑量低，只需 10 分鐘待核種衰變後即可進行下位病人治療，一天
治療人數可達 6 人以上，使用效率顯著優於 Sumitomo，可支撐多適應症商業化
與治療量規模化外，禾榮科致力提供以加速器型硼中子捕獲治療（AB-BNCT）為
核心的一條龍整體解決方案，公司橫跨「治療前精準評估、治療中高效計畫與定位、
標靶照射治療、治療後長效維護」四大階段，全面構築競爭對手難以跨越的技術與
行業壁壘。 
跨足全球與多元適應症為禾榮科中長期營運創造強大堆疊效應：目前單台 BNCT 設
備年治療容量可達 1,500 人，以台灣 BNCT 潛在市場年新增病患來約 5.5 萬人，
五年累積人數預估達 24 萬人，復發率 30-40%，設備需求量達超過 50 台，禾榮
科自 2024 年醫療器材取證以來，恩慈治療上已累 300 名以上病患，涵蓋近 30 項
腫瘤適應症，並在復發型頭頸癌、三陰性乳癌等多個適應症上展現優越療效，成功
吸引國內外指標性醫院展開深度合作。研究部認為 2026 年底取證後，2027 年起
BNCT 有機會以多重適應症收治病人。 
台灣市場：除已開展治療的中醫大及 1H27 預計商轉的北榮外，新竹竹銘與義大醫
院也預計於 2028 年接棒營運。此外，國內大型指標教學醫院正與公司進行乳癌臨
床試驗，未來可望深化合作範疇。 
海外市場：佈局正全面加速，包括日本合作方已有病患來台接受治療，東南亞市場
以新加坡為核心，除已於今年五月提出醫材註冊申請，公司近日也公告將以 13.8
億元入主新加坡上市公司 SAM Holdings(SGX：9G2)，取得 50.09%控制權，因
SAM 在新加坡擁有先進之質子治療中心與光子放射治療服務，雙方結合後，禾榮
科將導入自家加速器型硼中子捕獲治療（AB-BNCT）系統，打造東南亞首座「雙
粒子癌症治療平台」，預計 2028 年以醫療中心模式啟動治療，提供全方位精準腫
瘤治療服務，因新加坡具備高度國際化與健全的醫療法規體系，是連結東南亞醫療
市場的關鍵門戶，故未來將以新加坡為樞紐，全力搶攻東南亞與國際醫療商機，美
國市場則已與邁阿密、明尼蘇達、紐約三地指標醫院接洽，並於六月向美國 FDA 
圖二：禾榮科在行業中已建立強勁的技術壁壘

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

### Page 3

3

提出上市前諮詢申請。 
整體而言，禾榮科將自 2027 年起迎來營運轉捩點，正式從臨床驗證走向實質營收
兌現。隨著適應症拓展、區域市場放量、複合式療法推進至一線治療，禾榮科的全
球商業模式將加速複製，在設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利
將迎來跳躍式增長。 
2028 年達國產國造目標，設備交期有效掌握下，對業務發展將如虎添翼：禾榮科
AB-BNCT 治療系統目前 90%之子系統由禾榮科自主開發，為全面掌控設備交期、
供應鏈管理與確保關鍵材料之供應，禾榮科與 Advanced Cyclotron Systems Inc.
簽署加速器技術授權移轉案，並於 10/16 公告於苗栗購地打造高階醫療加速器研
發製造與藥物製造基地，整合設備製造、系統組裝、測試驗證與藥物開發能力，預
計 2028 年達到「國產國造」目標。研究部認為，相較於競爭者多以單一產品切入
市場，禾榮科具備「AB-BNCT 設備＋硼中子治療藥物＋治療計劃系統」的完整整
合能力，能為醫療院所提供一站式解決方案，形成明顯的差異化優勢。未來隨國產
自製設備問世，在設備建置時程可有效掌控的前提下，將有望進一步加速全球設備
的鋪設進度與國際業務拓展。 
財務預估：禾榮科在營收結構上，藥物部分將依病患數按比例收取治療費，設備則
採賣斷、BOT 與租賃等多元模式有不同認列方式，多管齊下加速全球布局並建立
可複製的癌症診療平台。獲利部分，由於藥物收入與治療收入的毛利率達 70-80%，
設備認列的毛利率約 60-70%，後續隨著藥物與治療比重增加，獲利率可望提升。 
預估禾榮科 2026 年營收 0.1 億元，稅後虧損 3.98 億元，稅後 EPS-2.52 元，2027
年在北榮設備可完全認列收入，中醫大及北榮開始進行正式病患治療收費，與南京
明碁醫院設備依完工比例認列營收下，預估營收將跳增至 29.6 億元，稅後淨利 16.1
億元，稅後 EPS 10.2 元；隨合作醫院持續進行設備商轉，預估 2028 年竹銘醫院、
義大醫院與新加坡 NUH 可望開始收治病患，營收預估達 41.2 億元(+39.3YoY)，
稅後淨利 22.1 億元(+39.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 13.94 元；2029 年預估裝機醫院陸續
商轉以及病患持續累積，經常性收入占比擴大，營收 53.9 億元(+30.8%YoY)，稅
後淨利 27.5 億元(+24.8%YoY)，稅後 EPS 17.39 元，後續每年隨著醫院設備裝機
與治療人數累積，獲利可望呈現每年成長。禾榮科的全球商業模式將加速複製，在
設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利將迎來跳躍式增長，建議買
進，目標價以 NPV 估算(WACC=6%，永續性獲利成長 10%)為 825 元。 
圖三：禾榮科全球市場拓展狀況

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

⻉莱德与 IFM 财团正重新评估 Stack Infrastructure 亚太数据中⼼资产，考虑下调约 200 亿
⾄ 250 亿美元企业价值对应的股权对价，并将部分付款与项⽬交付挂钩，引⼊业绩对赌。原
因是⾏业波动加剧、社区反对升温、 AI 算⼒需求前景分歧，投资者质疑运营商能否兑现扩
张计划。
摘要：
数据中⼼⾏业波动加剧，⻉莱德领衔财团正重新评估亚太区资产价值，考虑下调收购要约并引
⼊业绩对赌条款。
据彭博报道，⻉莱德（ BlackRock ）与 IFM Investors Pty 组成的投资财团正考虑下调对 Stack
Infrastructure 亚太区数据中⼼的收购报价，原因是该⾏业波动性上升，加之本地社区反对声⾳
渐强，算⼒需求前景不确定性加剧。
据知情⼈⼠透露，该财团可能削减要约中的股权对价部分，并将部分款项与项⽬实际交付情况
挂钩，实质上引⼊业绩对赌机制。此前，这批亚太区数据中⼼资产的企业价值估算介于 200 亿⾄
250 亿美元之间。
上述议价压⼒折射出数据中⼼⾏业更深层的担忧：投资者越来越质疑各⼤数据中⼼运营商能否

兑现其雄⼼勃勃的扩张计划，⽽ AI 算⼒需求能否持续⽀撑⾼估值，亦受到市场审视。
财团考虑调整要约结构
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠消息，⻉莱德旗下⼈⼯智能基础设施合伙基⾦（ Artificial Intelligence
Infrastructure Partnership ，简称 AIIP ）参与其中的投资财团，正与 Stack Infrastructure 资产持
有⽅ Blue Owl Capital 就收购条款展开磋商。双⽅已独家谈判约两个⽉，财团同期对相关资产进
⾏尽职调查。
知情⼈⼠表⽰，财团正在考虑的调整主要涉及两个层⾯：⼀是削减要约中的股权部分⾦额；⼆
是将部分付款与项⽬实际落地进度挂钩，⽽⾮⼀次性⽀付固定对价。上述调整尚未形成最终决
定，谈判仍在进⾏中。
⻉莱德与 IFM 的代表拒绝置评。 MGX 、 Blue Owl 及 Stack 均未回应置评请求。
亚太资产组合横跨五地
Stack Infrastructure 的亚太区资产是本次交易的核⼼。据 Stack 官⽹显⽰，相关数据中⼼分布于

东京、⼤阪、墨尔本、悉尼及⻢来西亚新⼭（ Johor Bahru ），覆盖⽇本、澳⼤利亚及东南亚三
⼤市场。
据彭博此前援引知情⼈⼠的消息，这批资产的企业价值估算区间为 200 亿⾄ 250 亿美元。知情⼈
⼠指出，扣除债务及资本⽀出后，要约中的股权对价部分将显著低于上述企业价值数字。
⾏业波动拖累资产定价
此次要约下调的背后，是数据中⼼⾏业估值逻辑正在承压。知情⼈⼠表⽰，本地社区对数据中
⼼项⽬的反对情绪持续升温，⽽市场对于 AI 相关算⼒需求的实际规模判断也愈发分歧，令资产估
值难度⼤幅上升。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking
the cash rate after the November increase. Since then, our
conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we
will publish the November Market Outlook with revised
forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing

market have all been revised down in 2027.
Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for
calendar 2027 more decisively inside the RBA’s 2–3% target
range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment
rate forecast has been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on
this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash rate from
here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.

A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The
housing market has undershot our forecasts over the past
couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been
coming in under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan
inquiries have taken another leg down in October after the
September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior
months, with the additional impact of the November rate hike
still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer.

Consumer income and spending have so far been quite
resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest
rates. But our Westpac Card Tracker is starting to show that
discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite
weak. We would not over-emphasise this point, but it does
imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB)

meeting, business survey data has shown weaker conditions
and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show
further evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-
labour cost shock that is not being fully passed through to
consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil
and fuel prices are already materially lower than the track
assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-
fourth lower than on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts

were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of
fuel costs to other prices, as well as easing consumer inflation
perceptions and expectations.
An RBA peak of greater conviction
Forecast changes
• A full update to our forecasts has been published in our
November Market Outlook.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
In the media conference following the November meeting,
Governor Bullock flagged that it was a good time to “take

stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the
market read this as a signal that the MPB would pause at the
December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to
downside risks than the internal members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is
looking even less likely given signs in the data and surveys.
The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and

senior officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a
downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though,
a rate hike in 2027 would require either a material new
inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would
constitute a policy error on the part of the RBA (something
that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the talents
and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with

the dissenting voters in the MPB that the downside risks need
more attention.
Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to
February, we think there will be more evidence that the
domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation
outlook with it.
“A deeper housing downturn and early signs of
cracks in the consumer sector point to an even
softer 2027."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
the labour market while showing significant concern over
the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the
passthrough of energy and technology input inflation. His
conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the monthly

rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward
supporting an increase in our policy rate of 25 basis points
at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI
inflation data out later today are similar to the past two
monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent
or even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also
kept the focus on inflation risks and stated or implied the FOMC

needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s
updates, pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January
meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by September. We believe
this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core
inflation ex shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-
month annualised basis and the labour market is balanced; but
it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate,

US households are under pressure from the cost of living, and
their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing
costs. Highlighting this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in
October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and
2010. Our November Market Outlook provide a full update on
our expectations for the US and the world.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index

fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest reading since
late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of
severe economic stress. Renewed cost-of-living and interest
rate pressures were front and centre following another lift
in inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the
RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage holders are feeling
the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group
falling to a record low. Consumers have become noticeably

more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the
relevant sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its
long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business
survey showed confidence slipped further to –15 in October as
conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic.
Of concern for the outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also
the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow
materially faster than selling prices, further compressing
already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter
financial conditions can jolt activity. The value of new dwelling
finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly
decline in the series’ history, taking approvals 25% lower year-
to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined

with the housing tax changes announced in May. Owner-
occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home
buyers proving particularly sensitive to the deterioration in
affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum
across the domestic economy, consistent with Westpac’s
expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside
activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our

expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the consensus
view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the
result, but core inflation was also marginally stronger than
forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the
past four months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look
to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost inflation through.
Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace,
gaining 0.3% in October.

The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated
by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor Waller prior to the
release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Post election, where are we left?
This week markets and the economy needed to digest
the General Election results, as well as data on how the
economy is tracking through the final months of the year. A
centre-right coalition looks more likely to ultimately prevail,

although it may be a while before we know for sure and
longer again before a stable government emerges. Watch
this space as paths to victory for the centre-left remain.
Economic data are consistent with a still-strong external
sector but a weaker domestically-generated growth
impulse. Our upcoming Economic Overview next week will
examine these trends in more depth.
The real star of the show this week was the outcome of the
2026 General Election, which delivered an election night lead

to the centre-right coalition of the National, ACT and NZ First
parties. The preliminary result was a 67-57 seat margin in an
enlarged 131 seat parliament. Hence the centre-right won on
election night with a slender one-seat margin, given that 66
seats are required to form a majority. Financial markets and
the business sector generally cheered this outcome given their
preference for a centre-right government. However, the margins
are tight and there is the potential for the result to switch around

when the final vote count becomes available on November 27.
National leads Labour by narrow margins in two electorates
(Wairarapa and Hamilton East) and could lose those seats
when all is said and done. Also, special votes tend to swing
more to the left than the right historically and hence could
boost the centre-left bloc. The star performer of the election
was the Opportunity Party which debuted in parliament with
7 seats. The centre-left would require their support to get

over the line in all scenarios, whereas the centre-right might or
might not need them depending on how the special votes and
final electorate results play out. There seems a clearer path to
victory for the centre-right – especially if they can come to an
agreement with Opportunity – but clients should be wary of
complacency (which we note may be a risk).
A further complication is that the election left the incumbent
National Party in a much-weakened position as their coalition

partners picked up seats at their expense, and because their
much-reduced share of the party vote (29.2%) meant that
no National Party list MPs made it into parliament. Several
seasoned and senior government ministers needed a list seat
to make it into the new government including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. Even if the final results confirm a centre-right
coalition win, there will be fierce debate within the coalition
on which party staffs key ministerial roles. ACT party leader

David Seymour is making a strong play for Finance Minister,
which Prime Minister Christopher Luxon is fiercely resisting.
There is the potential for tough and protracted coalition
negotiations to play out well into the Christmas period.
Indeed, I spoke on a post-election panel with an ex-National
Party senior minister this week who expressed the view that
it might not be until Waitangi Day (February 6) before a new
government is formed. Should anything like this play out then

the implications for economic and market uncertainty could
be significant. The initial relief rally in the New Zealand dollar
might prove premature in that scenario.
For interest rate markets, the main conclusion drawn is that as
a centre-left government seems a lower probability prospect,
then so also is the likelihood of a return to a ‘dual mandate’
for the RBNZ. Chief Economist Paul Conway made remarks
this week suggesting ongoing concern with inflation risks,

suggesting he is still in the hike camp at this stage. This has
seen markets firm up short-term OCR expectations such that a
25bp OCR increase in December is now seen as high probability
(85-90% currently). Bond markets cheered the prospect of
potentially tighter fiscal policy that could come with a centre-
right government. The spread between the 10-year government
bond and swap spread narrowed 4 basis points over the week
and the 10-year government bond yield differential to the US

fell to -49 basis points – the most negative since 2019.
Economic data showed further evidence of the two-speed
economy that is showing above-trend growth yet at the same
time feels quite unsatisfying to most voters. The low exchange
rate, generally robust global demand and tight supply
conditions in soft commodity markets have been delivering
consistently strong export returns for a couple of years now.
This week we saw another very strong GDT Pulse auction that

propelled whole milk powder futures higher – up some 20-25%
on year ago levels in USD terms. Significant upside risk for our
2026/27 Farmgate Milk Price forecast of $9.75/kgMS now exists,
which we will be delving into more deeply in our Economic
Overview to be released on Wednesday. The manufacturing
sector appears to remain in a solid state, with the manufacturing
PMI slipping a touch in October to 54.9 from 56.3 in September.
Short-term visitor arrivals have resumed their prior strong

uptrend, growing almost 3% for a second consecutive month
in September and almost 10% higher than a year earlier.
While arrivals from key European markets remain well below
year-earlier levels – likely reflecting the disruption to travel
through key Middle Eastern hubs along with higher jet fuel
prices – this has been more than offset by a very sharp lift in
arrivals from China. Indeed, Chinese arrivals in September
were almost two-thirds higher than the same month last year.

Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
And in the September quarter, arrivals from China surpassed
their pre-pandemic level for the first time. The number of
Australian citizens visiting New Zealand grew just under 6%yr
in September, but growth momentum from New Zealand’s
number one source market (a 30% share) does appear to have

slowed somewhat in recent months.
Less positive trends were evident in the more domestically
orientated areas of the economy. The most concrete indicator
of the NZ economy – concrete poured – dropped back a
touch in the September quarter, while consumer spending
in fell 0.7% in October. We have concerns on the quality
of the consumer spending data given that bank-sourced
data indicates a rise in consumer spending in October. But
nonetheless it’s probably reasonable to infer that higher fuel

costs in October crimped household budgets.
Housing market data for October were also week. House sales
fell and are down almost 22% y/y, while house prices fell in
October for the fifth consecutive month to be down almost
2% y/y. The weak performance of the housing market is likely
another factor weighing on consumer spending and residential
investment. That’s another topic we will be taking a look at in
next week’s Economic Overview.
Net migration flows for September confirm still modest

population growth. Arrivals of non-New Zealand citizens
continues to drift higher while departures declined a touch.
The net outflow of New Zealanders remains relatively high but
is declining, as signs of firmer employment growth emerge in
New Zealand at the same time as the Australian labour market
shows signs of weakening.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Nov 19, Last: –39.3, Westpac f/c: 30
Market f/c: 27.5, Range: 9.9 to 42
Employment fell by 39.3k in September, well below expectations
for a 20k lift. Some of this weakness may reflect an unwind of
a possible residual seasonality stemming from recent changes

to the survey design, which saw August come in stronger.
Under the monthly noise, the labour market is loosening
gradually as jobs growth runs slower than population growth.
While some leading indicators point to near-term stability
– such as job ads – business surveys and detail within the
employment data point to some downside risk.
For October, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of +30k,
which would see employment growth hold steady on a three-

month average basis.
AUS: Nov Westpac Housing Sentiment (index)
Nov 17, Last: 85.3
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index that appears in our
regular Housing Pulse reports will now be published on a
monthly basis a week after Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment is released. The Index is a composite
based on housing-related sentiment measures from the survey
and has proven to be an extremely useful predictor of turning
points in housing markets, both nationally and across states.

At 85.3, the Oct monthly Index read was holding near historic
lows, well below the long run avg of 100. The Nov update will
capture reactions to the RBA’s latest interest rate rise and the
deepening and broadening housing downturn. The release
will also include results from two new questions added to
the regular survey on consumers’ property buying and selling
intentions over the next twelve months.
AUS: Q3 Wage Price Index (%qtr)
Nov 18, Last: 0.8, Westpac f/c: 1.0

Market f/c: 1.0, Range: 0.9 to 1.1
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 0.8% in Q2, leaving annual
growth at 3.2%. Private sector wages continued to decelerate,
in line with a softer labour market, while public sector wages
provided a bit of an offset.
The Fair Work Commission's (FWC) 2026 Annual Wage Review
handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award
wages. This will have a noticeable impact on the Q3 WPI, as the
wage increases stemming from the review are typically passed

on during the September quarter.
We have pencilled in a rise of 1.0% for Q3, lifting the annual
pace to 3.4%yr. Detail on wages growth by bargaining stream
will be closely watched for evidence of broader diffusion
beyond arrangements directly linked to the FWC's decision.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Nov 19, Last: 4.7, Westpac f/c: 4.7
Market f/c: 4.7, Range: 4.6 to 4.8
Once again, the surprise in employment coincided with a surprise
in labour force participation, which fell 0.2ppts to 66.8% from
its near cycle high of 67.0% in August. This translated to a 26.2k

decline in the size of the labour force, which was less than the
drop in employment. As a result, the unemployment rate ticked
slightly higher in September, from 4.65% to 4.72% (although it
was steady at 4.7% to one decimal place), a new cycle high.
For October, we have the participation rate steady at 66.8%,
which together with the forecast rise in employment, would
see the unemployment rate also hold steady at 4.7%. Early
signs of rising retrenchments are a key signal to watch over the

coming months, especially if new hiring across other areas of
the economy starts to dry up.
NZ: Oct Selected Consumer Prices
Nov 17
Stats NZ’s monthly Selected Prices provides an update on
around half of the full CPI, including many of the more volatile
items. In terms of the big groups, we’re expecting a 0.1%
rise in overall food prices, with fresh produce prices typically
soft at this time of year. We also expect continued softness
in housing rents. However, the key focus will be on transport

costs. Petrol prices are expected to be up 11%. And we’ll be
watching to see if high global fuel prices are contributing to
further increases in airfares after recent large increases. There
is related uncertainty around holiday accommodation costs
(which drive much of the month-to-month volatility in overall
prices).
NZ: Q4 RBNZ Survey of Expectations (%yr)
Nov 17, Inflation 2yrs Ahead, Last: 2.34
With an easing in headline inflation in the September

quarter, we’re likely to see some easing in near-term inflation
expectations (one year ahead). However, longer term inflation
expectations, particularly the closely-watched two years
ahead measure, are likely to remain broadly steady. While
interest rates are moving higher, businesses are continuing to
report pressures on operating costs. In addition, despite their
recent easing, oil prices remain elevated. The only time longer-
term inflation expectations have moved materially below 2.3%

was in the mid-2010s. That followed an extended period when
inflation was running below 2%.

Document 325: Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-23)

Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)

Pages: 8

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
AI Implies Dramatic Shift in the Structure of the U.S. Economy
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
On November 16, Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh moderated three conference
sessions at a symposium focused on how AI will ripple through the economy. The event
was the first public look at one of the five task forces Warsh created to review the
Federal Reserve (Fed) processes for doing its job. The task forces are meant to help

reform the Fed by building a consensus for overhaul to address shortcomings Mr. Warsh
has raised about the Fed's performance over the years. The outsized role of AI in the
evolution of the U.S. economy is reflected in the task force to consider AI's impact on
employment and productivity. This is, perhaps, the least controversial of the various
issues the task forces are charged with considering but nevertheless one of major
importance.
The main takeaway from the symposium was that various scenarios remain possible

with quite different implications for the future of U.S. growth, employment and interest
rates. The wide range of outcomes has contributed to the recent volatility in equity
markets, especially the parts of the market most closely associated with the buildout
of AI data centers. Estimating the payoff from the buildout naturally remains a key
focus for investors but one that is subject to uncertainty because of the rapid pace of
technological innovation and the political pressures that are arising from public concerns

over the changes AI is bringing.
Exhibit 1 helps shed light on the dramatic consequences AI is likely to create for the
structure of the U.S. economy and employment. The cost structure of corporate output
as of 2025 includes about half of each dollar for labor. An estimate for the cost structure
of a dollar spent on inferential AI after the data center buildout reaches a net stock
centered around $5 trillion in 2030 requires only a fourth as much labor per unit of

revenue or output. The bigger the share of overall value inferential AI becomes in gross
domestic product (GDP) the lower the labor share of income will become. This is not a
new trend. The labor share of income has been gradually declining ever since the digital
economy began to develop over a half century ago. What is new is the speed of the
transformation. Self-generative AI is representative of the singularity, the point when the
spread of this digital transformation becomes exponential.

The drastically lower labor share of value implied by AI output implies that other costs
comprise bigger shares. For example, the exhibit shows the profit share of revenue is
much higher (28% vs.19%) for AI-generated output. This is already evident in the profit
profile of the S&P 500 where the margins and profit growth of the AI-related companies
are orders of magnitude greater than the profit profiles of non-AI-related businesses.
Presumably, as the old economy increasingly transforms to this new economy cost

structure, the productivity benefits of AI will increasingly transfer over to traditional
businesses and transform their cost structures in the direction of those of inferential AI
builders. This analysis suggests profit growth is moving toward a bigger share of output
over the next few years.
Competition naturally eats away at "excess" profits, those not justified by costs. Exhibit 1
partly illustrates the justification for higher AI profits. AI is capital-intensive compared

to the non-AI economy. This can be seen in the much higher depreciation-cost share
in the AI economy (28% vs.15%). AI capital is more short-lived than average, reflecting
the quicker obsolescence that naturally results from accelerating technological change
and less durable infrastructure (short-lived chips). This issue of rapid depreciation has
surfaced in the debate over potential AI profitability, with analysts trying to figure the
right life span of data centers to model their profit outlook.

Investment Implications
AI's creative destruction is likely to
leave a lot of laggards in its wake while
creating a stronger, more profitable
economy for investors. As a result,
we believe a diversified investment
approach favoring high-quality balance
sheets and reliable free cash flows is
warranted.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Finally, "other costs" make up about a third of AI output cost compared to just 12% for
non-AI output. Among these are the increased power costs associated with intensive
compute processing. Electricity costs are going to replace a lot of labor costs. As the
midterms made clear, rules and regulation for data centers, including power and water
issues, are likely to come under much greater scrutiny as well as other environmental and

"quality-of-life" issues.
Ultimately, this new cost structure for the AI economy will be the focus of innovation
to bring down costs and remove bottlenecks. Technology is ultimately deflationary.
Monetary policy is the cause of inflation. This "deflationary" or declining cost aspect
of technological progress is a key factor behind the notion that the promise of the
singularity is a world of superabundance. Intelligence, power and compute costs are likely

to become cheaper and cheaper as innovators work to create this superabundance.

Employment and AI. The dramatic reduction in labor needed to run an AI cost structure
promises a much higher level of productivity and therefore higher levels of wealth and
income. As the cost structure in Exhibit 1 illustrates, however, a much bigger share
of output goes to capital and therefore shareholders in an AI-dominated economic
structure. There are various ways this change could sort itself out. In a society with an
increasingly older population, more capital income solves the problem of more retirees

needing to live on investment income. AI also promises to increase longevity. Therefore,
the future is likely to include more elderly people living on capital income as well as
more elderly people working. In addition, the bounty from higher productivity is likely
to support demand for new goods and services as past technology breakthroughs have
shown. People will be required to produce those new goods and services.
Nevertheless, it's clear from the distribution of capital income that's becoming more, not

less, concentrated, that policies that spread wealth ownership across a broader swath
of the population could potentially help ease the social tensions plaguing the population
under the guise of "affordability." The proliferation of gifts from the wealthy to Trump
Accounts is a reflection of the recognition that this issue needs to be addressed. The
U.S. Treasury rolled out temporary regulations at the end of September that allow Trump
Accounts to hold donated individual stocks instead of just diversified index funds. There

is likely to be a push to expand the growth of large philanthropic donations from the
private sector to fund retirement-type accounts for the broader population. This is an
alternative to higher tax rates that fund government spending rather than long-term
saving. A society that shares more broadly in the fruits of AI is more likely to tolerate AI's
profound changes.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Too Connected to Fail: When the Jetsons Meet the Flintstones
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
This month, when a leading U.S. technology company went dark, planes across America
were grounded, and major banks went offline, investors learned an unsettling lesson on
the fragility of America’s digital economy.  We learned that interconnectivity is both a

strength and a weakness—and that the digital economy of the future rests upon the
shaky infrastructure of the past.
We live in a world where AI is advancing at warp speed. Computing power is expanding
exponentially. Financial transactions move around the globe in milliseconds. And cloud
computing keeps billions of people and businesses connected every day. It is a world
where everything is faster, digital and connected.
The U.S. digital economy now accounts for roughly 12% of GDP, expanding about three

times faster than the economy overall this century. Digital gross output totaled $3.7
trillion in 2021, the last year of available data from the Commerce Department; that said,
the figure almost certainly understates the current size of the U.S. digital economy. Digital
commerce now accounts for nearly 18% of all retail sales ($350 billion), although again,
this number is understated. Add in business-to-business digital transactions and digitally

delivered professional services, and the amount of daily digital commerce in the U.S. is
massive and growing robustly. Each one of these transactions ultimately depends on
communications services, electricity, computing facilities, and digital payments networks.
Yet here’s the rub: our digital world is juxtaposed against a physical infrastructure
of aging radar systems, antiquated telecommunications equipment, half-century-old
transmission lines, vulnerable underwater subsea cables and decades-old software.

Here we are, well into the 21st century, and a handful of Federal Aviation Administration
facilities are still using floppy disks to support aviation travel. Our world is a world where
the space-age Jetsons have collided with the Stone Age Flintstones.
Something had to give, and it did this November: business communications were
disrupted, planes were grounded, ATMs were frozen and investors were left wondering
“what’s next”?
In our opinion, “what’s next” entails a renewed focus and commitment on the part of the

U.S. public and private sectors to build America’s physical infrastructure back better and
faster. The recent disruptions, we believe, are a reminder that a nation’s infrastructure is
essentially its backbone, and every nation, like every human being, is only as strong as its
skeletal framework.
A healthy backbone allows an economy to move, pivot, grow, innovate and compete. An
impaired spine, in contrast, constrains mobility, undermines nimbleness and flexibility and

is prone to setbacks.
So, think of roads, bridges, ports, airports, electrical grids, communication networks and,
increasingly, digital networks not merely as conveniences of modern life. Rather, think
of them as foundational components of economic activity, national security and global
competitiveness. Without them, even the world’s most sophisticated economy can be
brought to a standstill.
The great irony—and potential opportunity—of the “too connected to fail” is that while the

U.S. leads the world in AI, cloud computing, and other advanced technological capabilities,
the physical infrastructure of the nation continues to show its age. Electrical grids are
struggling to accommodate increasing demand. Transmission networks are plagued with
bottlenecks. Aviation communications systems built in the 1970s are overwhelmed.
None of the above is new—America’s infrastructure deficiencies have been widely
acknowledged but frequently tolerated for decades. The grid held up most of the time,

while airline safety remained relatively stellar. Power outages during storms and heat
waves were expected and tolerated—no matter the misery created. All good.
Investment Implications
The future is digital, which
requires more capital investment
on America’s aging physical
infrastructure. We continue to favor
Industrials, infrastructure-related
firms, and commodities for the long-
run.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Today, however, it’s different. Because there is so much riding on our digital
connectivity—think trillions of dollars of daily commerce—the cost of failure or
disruption continues to soar. Case in point: America’s recent aviation meltdown. The
communications malfunction triggered a national four-hour ground stop, resulting in
nearly 20,000 flight cancellations over four days, at a potential cost to the airlines

of more than $1 billion in profits. Adding more salt to the wound, fried and frizzled
passengers absorbed an estimated $1.9 billion in additional economic costs. All because
the communications infrastructure supporting the world’s most sophisticated aviation
system failed to handle a technical upgrade.
Where are the Jetsons when you need them? We believe the recent technological failures
will accelerate infrastructure spending for a number of reasons. One, as shown above,

the economic costs of digital failures are rising. Two, America’s digital economy is now
too big—and too connected—to fail. Three, the extraordinary power needed to run
the AI revolution makes it absolutely essential that the U.S. stop talking about fixing
its aging infrastructure and start doing it. Four, with the stakes so large for America’s
largest technology companies, with enormous incentives of their own, these firms are
contributing their private capital of their own to build back America’s infrastructure

faster and better. And fifth, an aging air traffic control system, an unreliable national
grid, and weathered transmission lines―these are more than just economic and financial
challenges. They are challenges to America’s national security—notably to the great
power rivalry between the U.S. and China. This rivalry, in our opinion, will help create
more bipartisan support for increased infrastructure spending and greater public-private
sector cooperation.

Investment summary. It’s taken a series of technological snafus, but here we are—
America’s infrastructure is gaining the attention—and capital—it needs. It’s also
garnered the attention of Congress. Just last week, the Bipartisan American Affordability
Act, which reduces permitting burdens on a number of projects, passed a key procedural
vote in the Senate. Whether the House votes in favor of the bill remains to be seen but
in general, the U.S.’s cumbersome permitting and regulatory systems are coming under

more scrutiny.
To this point, the bipartisan National Governors Association recently outlined
recommendations designed to cut through the permitting and regulatory red tape. The
Department of Energy has developed a coordinated federal transmission permitting
framework designed to clear and approve projects at a faster pace. Yes, it will take
some time to cut through the labyrinth of federal, state and local approvals, permits, and
regulations. But the need for reforms has become quite evident and urgent, with political

inaction less acceptable to an electorate increasingly worried and wary of the next
technology glitch.
So, who potentially benefits in our theme of “too connected to fail”?  In our opinion,
the winners will include electrical equipment manufacturers, engineering firms, utilities,
telecommunications infrastructure providers, industrial automation companies, and
specialized construction firms. Specialists in maritime surveillance, underwater sonar
detection, and fiber-optics sensing capabilities are well positioned in this new world.

In terms of asset allocation, this means considering more exposure to infrastructure-
related industrial companies and leaders in renewables (solar, wind, electric vehicles,
biomass) and the required infrastructure behind each renewable energy source. Leaders
in electricity distribution, charging stations, batteries, as well as low-carbon hydrogen,
biomethane, and advanced biofuels should also be considered in portfolios. Ditto for
leaders in low-carbon technologies (LED lighting, smart energy meters, and storage),

and leaders in transmission technologies like high-voltage direct current (HVDC), which
transfers wind and solar power from where it is created to where it is needed.  We also
still favor commodities over the long term, as well as cybersecurity leaders.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
In our view, what does the market want and need to see to establish a firmer base?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
IInvestors are looking to reprice uncertainty for 2027. A lot has been digested by this
market since semiconductor stocks peaked in June. Inflation, Fed policy, geopolitics,
the funding of the AI buildout, CEO changes and future power adjustments out of the

midterm elections all in a matter of months.
Most importantly, investors kept clinging to the base—earnings and profitability. As long
as the base remained strong and growing, investors were willing to take the risk of a
market led by technology and the AI buildout more specifically.
Recently, investors have shifted to wanting more certainty: certainty around higher-
quality, transparency in FCF, the ability to secure financing if needed, reasonable

valuations and strong moats that last beyond peak profitability. This is why we have
experienced a shift from the more highly cyclical semiconductors to the hyperscalers in
terms of positioning.
The market wants to see that AI spending remains robust but with more reasonable
expectations for 2027 and perhaps 2028. A reset of expectations by the leaders of
the buildout can go a long way to finalizing this “flush” stage, in our view. We remain
diversified Equity and are proud Bulls overall.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading
companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is the equal-weight version of the widely-used S&P 500, in which each constituent company is given the same weight (approximately 0.2% each), rather than
weighting by market capitalization.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

DO ITALIAN HOUSEHOLDS HAVE WHAT IT TAKES TO FINANCE EUROPE’S NEXT IDEAS?
Andrea d’Aquino
Between 2019 and 2025, US value added grew at twice the pace of Europe’s, driven largely by technology and labour productivity 1,2. Yet Europe
does not lack saving to finance a similar development: over the past ten years, Euro area gross national saving exceeded domestic investment
by about €300 bn a year, equivalent to 15% of productive investment 3, and in 2024-2025 gross capital outflows to the United States averaged

€785 billion a year 4. The situation described therefore creates a “European paradox”, where household particularly save but do not take on so
much risk to foster innovation as in the US.
Safety versus risk: how Italian and European households differ
In Q2 2026, Euro area households’ gross financial wealth grew by 6.5% y/y to approximately €37 trillion, of which just over 30% is held in
currency and deposits (€11,165 billion), compared with about 10% in the United States. Italy has the lowest share of currency and deposits

among the major Euro area economies (see Chart 1). In Q2 2026, Italian households’ financial wealth grew by 6.3% y/y, in line with the
Euro area, reaching €6,609 billion, of which 24.2% is held in currency and deposits (about €1,598 billion), compared with 28.3% in France
(€2,151 billion), 32% in Spain (€1,168 billion) and 36% in Germany (€3,660 billion).
1 Fabio Panetta; Il risparmio nella società che cambia, Banca d’Italia; 30th October 2026.
2 Luisa Carpinelli et al.; The Great Divide: productivity dynamics in the United States and euro area after the pandemic; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 924, Banca d’Italia; April

2025.
3 Productive investments are gross fixed investments minus residential construction.
4 It includes the item ‘other investments’, mainly made up of deposits, loans, and trade receivables.
SOURCE: EUROSTAT, BNL ECONOMIC RESEARCH
In Italy the largest share of households’ total financial assets is invested in equity
and investment fund shares, less in currency and deposits

Chart of the Week / 18 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Italian risk is tied up mostly in unlisted firms
Judging by this metric, European households’ risk appetite is lower than that of their US counterparts. Another metric to look at: the listed
shares. They account for 5% of Euro area gross financial wealth 5 (3.1% in Italy, 3.7% in France, 6.6% in Spain, 7.2% in Germany). However,
once holdings in unlisted companies are included, the share of risk capital rises to 15% in the Euro area, 34% in the United States and

more than 20% in Italy.
A large part of the gap reflects the wider presence in Europe of unlisted firms, largely family-owned. In Italy in particular, unlisted shares
account for 17.1% of gross financial wealth, just over 7 pp above the Euro area average, compared with 12.4% in France, 7.7% in Spain and
2.6% in Germany (see Chart 2). Unlisted family firms are on average smaller and less productive than the system as a whole, especially
among medium-sized and large firms and in technology- and knowledge-intensive sectors 6.

Italian households’ risk capital is therefore largely held in forms that are hard to diversify and in firms far from the technological frontier,
while the risk capital reaching the youngest firms (less than 12 years old) is scarce, even though these firms are very active in innovation:
innovative start-ups account for just over 2% of young firms but hold about 44% of that category’s patents. Similarly, venture-capital-
backed firms are barely 0.2% of young firms but account for around 12% of total patents of the youngest firms 7.

Italian inheritances will largely pass on bricks
Investment choices are also linked to demographics: risk appetite tends to rise until adulthood and then declines with advancing age 8.
According to Banca d’Italia 9, in the coming years in Italy, those born between 1966 and 1975 will benefit from wealth transfers to a greater
extent than earlier cohorts: inheritances would account for around 40% of their wealth, compared with 30% for those born between 1951

and 1965. All else being equal, households expecting to receive an inheritance hold more financial assets. What will change hands is
mainly real assets, above all housing: in 2022 (latest available data), households whose reference person was born between 1941 and
1950 held on average around 62% of their total wealth in dwellings, 28% in financial assets and almost 10% in equity stakes in businesses.
As a result, once households receive the inheritance, their portfolios could become more tilted towards housing, which may lead to a

rebalancing towards financial assets.
The challenge for the Euro area in the coming years will be to press ahead with the creation of a single market for capital, in order to
provide greater investment opportunities and avert the “European savings paradox”.
andrea.aquino@bnpparibas.com
5 The US data is not available for listed share only.
6 Michele Cascarano et al.; Le imprese familiari e i passaggi generazionali in Italia; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 1068, Banca d’Italia; October 2026.

7 Maria Giulia Cassinis et al.; Innovative firms unveiled: economic and financial insight from Italian start-ups; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 967, Banca d’Italia; October 2025.
8 Raffaella Giordano et al.; Population ageing and portfolio choices in the main EU countries; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 1036, Banca d’Italia; July 2026.
